ABOUT THIS VOLUME

This is volume 5 of five, and it continues the catalogue at Chapter 240.

The front matter and Part One, the general account of the neuron, are given in Volume 1 and are not repeated here.

The tags and flags used throughout are explained in Volume 1, and are summarised again in one line each below.

• (PUC) marks a Previously Unnamed Construct: a neuron type that is real, functionally necessary and relied upon in the literature, but which had no accepted proper name until this work gave it one.

• (COG) marks a type involved in cognition, and (NON-COG) marks a type that is not.


PART FIFTEEN: DORSAL ROOT GANGLION NEURONS - BODY SENSATION (CONTINUED FROM VOLUME 4)

CHAPTER 239. LOW-THRESHOLD MECHANORECEPTORS (LTMRS) (Gentle-Touch Sensing Neuron) (NON-COG)

Every pleasant thing your skin has ever told you arrived on one of these wires.

Low-threshold mechanoreceptor, abbreviated LTMR, is laboratory shorthand describing a measurement setting rather than a purpose, so it teaches a student nothing.

Gentle names the defining property, because these cells fire to skin indentations far below the force needed to cause harm, in the fraction of a millinewton range, unlike their high-threshold neighbours.

Touch names the faculty they build.

Sensing Neuron marks them as the inward-reporting side of the nervous system rather than the commanding side, so that together the name says this is the family that lets a light contact be felt at all.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

A low threshold mechanoreceptor (LTMR) is a dorsal root ganglion (DRG) sensory neuron that fires to gentle deformation of the skin, far below any force that hurts.

Low threshold means it answers a push under one thousandth of a newton, roughly the weight of a grain of salt.

This entry is the parent family, and the four that follow are its fastest members, so their counts sit inside this one rather than adding to it.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Pseudounipolar, a round dendrite free cell body on a stalk that splits into a skin branch and a spinal branch.

The skin end is rarely a naked wire, ending instead in a corpuscle, on a Merkel cell, or as a palisade of bare membrane called a lanceolate ending within 100 nanometres of a hair follicle wall.

SIZE:

Fast myelinated members have cell bodies of median diameter 90 to 100 micrometres in the 2025 human atlas, while unmyelinated C tactile members fall between 42 and 80 micrometres.

Fibre diameter varies thirty fold, from 0.4 to 12 micrometres.

Human hair follicle LTMRs conduct in the fast A beta range, whereas the mouse equivalents conduct in the slower A delta range.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

• A beta slowly adapting types 1 and 2, and rapidly adapting types 1 and 2, each detailed in the four entries that follow.

• A beta field and hair follicle endings, which cover hairy skin.

• A delta down hair endings, the most sensitive of all, also excited by moving cool air.

• C tactile afferents, unmyelinated and tuned to slow stroking near 3 centimetres per second, which is the physiology of a caress.

The 2025 atlas resolved four A beta clusters, A-beta-LTMR.ETV1, A-beta-LTMR.LGI, A-beta-LTMR.NSG2 and A-beta-LTMR.CCKAR, plus a C-LTMR.CDH9 cluster.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Cell bodies fill dorsal root ganglia at every spinal level, and endings tile the body surface, both glabrous skin such as palm and sole and hairy skin everywhere else.

Central branches climb the dorsal columns to the gracile nucleus for the lower body and the cuneate nucleus for the upper body, dropping collaterals into laminae III to V, with C tactile fibres ending in inner lamina II.

The interface that matters is the dorsal root entry zone, where peripheral myelin gives way to central myelin.

NUMBER:

The best modern estimate is about 230,000 tactile afferent fibres in young adult skin, plausible range 200,000 to 270,000, falling 5 to 8 percent per decade.

About 19 percent serve the face and lips through the trigeminal ganglion, leaving roughly 185,000 with DRG cell bodies, of which about 60 percent are slowly adapting.

Adding hair follicle, field, deep tissue and joint mechanoreceptors gives a family of 250,000 to 400,000 cells, some 2.5 to 4 percent of the roughly 10 million DRG neurons and about 0.0003 percent of all neurons.

Confidence is high for the 230,000 figure and low for the wider bound, and single cell surveys assign LTMRs a larger share than these fibre counts allow, a discrepancy not yet resolved.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Fast myelinated members rest near minus 60 millivolts and fire spikes lasting half a millisecond to a millisecond with a very high safety factor.

C tactile members have broad spikes carried partly by tetrodotoxin resistant sodium channels and creep along at half a metre per second, so a stroke on the calf registers a full second late.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Central terminals excite dorsal column nucleus cells and laminae III to V neurons including a well known PKC gamma population.

They also excite inhibitory interneurons that damp incoming pain traffic, which is the basis of gate control and the reason rubbing a banged elbow helps.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

A beta fibres are 6 to 12 micrometres conducting at 33 to 75 metres per second, A delta fibres 1 to 5 micrometres at 5 to 30, and C fibres 0.4 to 1.2 micrometres at 0.5 to 2.

Myelin is shed at a heminode just before the ending, and that heminode is where spikes are born.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Glutamate throughout, packaged by VGLUT1 in myelinated members and by VGLUT3 in C tactile afferents.

Healthy LTMRs carry no substance P, but after nerve injury some A beta cells switch it on, and that switch is one route by which touch begins to hurt.

MYELINATION:

A beta and A delta members are myelinated one Schwann cell per internode, with thinner sheaths than proprioceptors.

C tactile afferents are unmyelinated, several axons sharing one Schwann cell in a Remak bundle.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Moderate but relentless, supporting axons up to a metre long.

The unbarriered capillaries of the ganglion let toxins and high blood glucose reach these cells freely, which is why diabetes and chemotherapy blunt touch early.

PLASTICITY:

Corpuscles and Merkel cells are rebuilt from stem cells for life, so the sensor is replaceable while the neuron is not.

After injury the cord becomes sensitised and light touch is read as pain, a PIEZO2 dependent state called mechanical allodynia, which is why sunburned skin hurts under a shirt.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Satellite glial cells jacket the cell body and buffer potassium exactly as for other DRG neurons.

The extraordinary difference is at the far end, where terminal Schwann cells, which elsewhere merely insulate, become the stacked lamellar cells that build Meissner and Pacinian corpuscles, so here the glia are the sense organ itself.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Take these cells out and skin still burns, aches and feels hot and cold, but it stops being informative.

Patients lacking PIEZO2 show exactly this: no vibration sense, no touch discrimination, no felt joint position, yet pain and temperature intact, so they cannot button a shirt in the dark.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

The sensor is PIEZO2, tuned by the accessory protein STOML3 and modulated by membrane cholesterol.

Much of the computation happens outside the membrane, because the capsule filters the stimulus mechanically before the neuron sees it, so each subtype is defined as much by its jelly and collagen as by its channels.

The T junction low pass filters traffic on the way in, and after injury these cells release factors that recruit microglia, turning a touch pathway into a pain pathway.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: indentation depth in micrometres, indentation velocity in micrometres per second, lateral skin strain as a fraction, vibration amplitude and frequency in micrometres and hertz.

• OUTPUTS: 0 to about 500 spikes per second, glutamate in the dorsal column nuclei and spinal laminae II to V, plus drive to gate control interneurons.

• STATE: adaptation level per subtype, refractory timer, spike phase accumulator, injury sensitisation flag.

• PARAMETERS: threshold 0.01 to 15 micrometres by subtype, gain 0.4 to 9 spikes per second per micrometre, conduction 0.5 to 75 metres per second.

• TIME CONSTANTS: transduction under 1 millisecond, adaptation 2 milliseconds to 10 seconds across the family, corpuscle renewal weeks, injury sensitisation hours to days.

• FAILURE MODE: diabetic and chemotherapy neuropathy strip the longest fibres first, giving numb feet with intact pain; PIEZO2 loss abolishes discriminative touch outright; after nerve injury the same input drives allodynia, where a bedsheet feels like a blade.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to convert one skin indentation trace into the firing rate of every low threshold mechanoreceptor class in a supplied list.
Create a number variable called adapted with initial value 0.0.
For each class in the class list, perform the following operations.
    Set adapted to 0.0.
    For each depth in the indentation trace, perform the following operations.
        Calculate the difference of depth and adapted.
        Set drive to the result.
        Calculate the quotient of 0.001 divided by the time constant of the class.
        Calculate the product of drive and the result.
        Calculate the sum of adapted and the result.
        Set adapted to the result.
        Calculate the difference of drive and the threshold of the class.
        Set rate to the result.
        Check if rate is less than 0.
        If that condition is true, then perform the following actions.
            Set rate to 0.
        End the conditional block.
        Calculate the product of rate and the gain of the class.
        Set rate to the result.
        Display rate to the console.
    End the loop.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

# Python: one tiny model with four parameter sets shows the whole family is a
# single leaky filter running at four different speeds.
DT = 0.001  # simulation step in seconds

# name: (gain in spikes/s per micrometre, adaptation time constant in s,
#        threshold in micrometres)
TYPES = {"SA1": (1.10, 4.000, 8.0), "SA2": (0.45, 10.000, 15.0),
         "RA1": (2.20, 0.060, 5.0), "RA2": (9.00, 0.002, 0.5)}

def response(name, depth_series):
    gain, tau, thresh = TYPES[name]
    adapted, phase, spikes = 0.0, 0.0, 0   # slow state, phase, spike count
    for depth in depth_series:
        drive = depth - adapted
        adapted = adapted + (depth - adapted) * DT / tau
        phase = phase + gain * max(0.0, drive - thresh) * DT
        while phase >= 1.0:                # emit whole spikes only
            phase, spikes = phase - 1.0, spikes + 1
    return spikes

if __name__ == "__main__":
    step = [0.0] * 50 + [100.0] * 500      # 100 um step held for 500 ms
    for name in ("SA1", "SA2", "RA1", "RA2"):
        print("%s fired %d spikes to a 100 um step" % (name, response(name, step)))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

It answers a force too small to bend a hair, and never answers heat, cold or damage.

It is the only sensory family whose end organs are built out of glial cells, and the only one whose loss leaves pain intact while making the world untouchable.

CHAPTER 240. A-BETA SLOWLY ADAPTING TYPE 1 (SA1) (Fine-Edge Pressure Neuron) (NON-COG)

Run a fingertip across a line of braille, and the cell reading it is only half a neuron, because its business end is plugged into a tiny clear cell borrowed from your skin.

SA1 is an alphanumeric series label recording only how fast the response decays in a laboratory recording.

Fine-Edge names what the cell is actually for, because its narrow, sharply bounded receptive field makes it the afferent that resolves corners, edges, curvature and raised dot patterns, and it is the channel that carries braille and fine surface form.

Pressure names the physical quantity it holds onto, since its partner Merkel cells transduce the sustained component of an indentation rather than only its onset.

Neuron marks it as a first-order afferent, and the name therefore teaches both the stimulus it keeps reporting and the perceptual job that reporting serves.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

An A beta slowly adapting type 1 afferent (SA1) is a fast myelinated dorsal root ganglion (DRG) neuron whose skin ending clamps onto Merkel cells, small transparent cells in the deepest layer of the epidermis.

Slowly adapting means it keeps firing while the skin stays pressed, instead of falling silent after the first moment.

Its speciality is spatial detail: edges, corners, points, curvature and printed dots, which is why braille is readable at all.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Pseudounipolar, with a round dendrite free cell body and a T shaped branch point.

The skin branch divides into up to about twenty twigs, each ending in a disc that indents one Merkel cell like a thumb pressed into dough.

In hairless skin the Merkel cells cluster at the tips of the epidermal ridges that make your fingerprint, and in hairy skin they cluster in a touch dome beside a guard hair.

SIZE:

Median cell body diameter is 90 to 100 micrometres in the 2025 human DRG atlas, and the fibre is 6 to 12 micrometres thick with myelin.

A Merkel cell is only 10 to 15 micrometres across, so a neuron a metre long ends on a target a hundred thousand times shorter than itself.

The receptive field, the skin patch that drives the cell, averages about 11 square millimetres on the fingertip, the smallest on the body.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

• Glabrous SA1 units, on Merkel cells at the bases of the epidermal ridges of fingertip, palm and sole.

• Hairy skin touch dome units, which give a brisker initial burst.

• Nail bed Merkel units, packed around the nail, helping encode fingertip force.

Assignment of these classes to the four A beta molecular clusters of the 2025 atlas is still provisional.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Cell bodies occupy dorsal root ganglia at every spinal level, with face equivalents in the trigeminal ganglion.

Endings live in the basal layer of the epidermis, at the interface between epidermis and dermis, an unusual address, since most touch endings stop in the dermis below.

Central branches reach laminae III, IV and V and climb the dorsal columns unbroken to the gracile and cuneate nuclei, crossing the dorsal root entry zone where Schwann cell myelin is handed to oligodendrocyte myelin.

NUMBER:

Take the whole body figure of about 230,000 tactile afferents, remove the roughly 19 percent that are facial, and apply the one quarter share SA1 units hold in hand recordings.

That gives about 50,000 SA1 neurons, plausible range 30,000 to 70,000, which is 0.5 percent of the roughly 10 million DRG neurons and about 0.00006 percent of all neurons.

It sits inside the LTMR family total rather than adding to it, at moderate confidence, anchored on Johansson and Vallbo's 241 tactile units per square centimetre at the fingertip against 58 in the palm.

Independent estimates put SA1 density near 70 to 100 units per square centimetre at the fingertip.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Resting voltage near minus 60 millivolts, with a narrow spike and no shoulder on its falling phase.

The signature is an irregular sustained discharge, and that raggedness is the classic test separating SA1 from SA2.

Rates run 20 to 60 spikes per second during a steady press and 100 to 200 during the ramp in, from a threshold near 8 to 30 micrometres, conducting at 35 to 75 metres per second.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Peripherally the Merkel cell is presynaptic to the neuron and releases serotonin onto it, so a synapse sits inside the receptor itself.

Centrally the terminal releases glutamate in laminae III to V and in the cuneate and gracile nuclei, where lateral inhibition sharpens the edge signal a second time.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Fast and thickly myelinated, branching heavily only at its two ends, with myelin shed at the last heminode before each disc.

Each of the twenty or so discs can start spikes independently, so one axon polls a small committee of sensors, which is why the receptive field has multiple hot spots.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Glutamate centrally, loaded by VGLUT1, and serotonin travelling the other way from Merkel cell to nerve terminal inside the receptor.

No substance P and no calcitonin gene related peptide, the two pain peptides, which is a defining negative marker.

Maintenance depends on neurotrophin 3 through the receptor TrkC and on the receptor RET.

MYELINATION:

Myelinated by Schwann cells peripherally and oligodendrocytes centrally, with a thinner sheath than a proprioceptor and nodes of Ranvier 0.5 to 1 millimetre apart.

The neuron instructs sheath thickness by displaying neuregulin-1 type III.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

High and sustained, because unlike most touch cells this one fires continuously while contact lasts.

The unmyelinated terminal discs pump sodium constantly and are packed with mitochondria, and the ganglion's absent blood nerve barrier lets glucose and platinum drugs reach the soma unfiltered.

PLASTICITY:

The sensor is renewable although the neuron is not, because Merkel cells are replaced from epidermal stem cells over weeks to months.

Merkel numbers fall with age, contributing to the decline in tactile spatial acuity after the fifth decade.

Braille reading expands the cortical territory these cells feed, and expert readers show finer acuity in the reading finger.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Satellite glial cells jacket the cell body and buffer the potassium a continuously firing neuron releases.

The unusual partner is at the far end, where the Merkel cell is not glia but a skin cell of epidermal origin recruited as a transducer, depending on the transcription factor ATOH1 for its existence.

Keratinocytes surround the complex and transmit force to it, while terminal Schwann cells stop at the epidermal boundary and leave the last stretch bare.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Delete SA1 and the fingertip becomes a pressure gauge that cannot read.

You would still know something was touching you and roughly how hard, because RA1 and SA2 survive, but shape would be gone: no edges, no corners, no letters, no telling a key from a coin without looking.

Braille would be a blank page, and the cuneate nucleus would lose the high resolution map it exists to sharpen.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

This is a two cell sensor with a clear division of labour.

The Merkel cell carries PIEZO2 and depends on it absolutely, producing the sustained response, while the nerve terminal carries its own PIEZO2 and produces the fast dynamic part, so one afferent runs two transducers at two speeds.

Fingerprint ridges focus strain onto the Merkel clusters, so skin architecture does part of the computation, and Merkel cells release peptides that influence local immune cells and keratinocytes.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: indentation depth in micrometres, useful range 8 to 1500; local surface curvature in reciprocal millimetres; indentation velocity in micrometres per second; skin temperature in degrees Celsius, which shifts gain slightly.

• OUTPUTS: glutamate in laminae III to V and in the cuneate and gracile nuclei, as an irregular train of 0 to about 200 spikes per second.

• STATE: membrane voltage, Merkel cell calcium, slow adaptation variable, PIEZO2 inactivation level, interval jitter seed.

• PARAMETERS: threshold 8 to 30 micrometres, static gain about 1 spike per second per micrometre, edge gain about 6 times static gain, receptive field about 11 square millimetres, conduction 35 to 75 metres per second.

• TIME CONSTANTS: transduction under 1 millisecond, fast adaptation 5 to 15 milliseconds, slow adaptation 2 to 5 seconds, Merkel cell turnover weeks.

• FAILURE MODE: PIEZO2 loss of function abolishes fine form perception while pain remains; diabetic and chemotherapy neuropathy kill the longest SA1 axons first; leprosy strips these endings from the cooler skin of the fingers; ageing thins the Merkel population.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to convert a scanned surface profile into the edge enhanced, slowly adapting discharge of an SA1 afferent.
Create a number variable called adaptation with initial value 0.0.
Create a number variable called previous_depth with initial value 0.0.
Create a number variable called older_depth with initial value 0.0.
For each centre_depth in surface_profile, perform the following operations.
    Calculate the sum of older_depth and centre_depth.
    Calculate the difference of the result and previous_depth.
    Calculate the difference of the result and previous_depth.
    Set curvature to the result.
    Check if curvature is less than 0.
    If that condition is true, then perform the following actions.
        Set curvature to 0.
    End the conditional block.
    Calculate the difference of previous_depth and 10.0.
    Calculate the difference of the result and adaptation.
    Set drive to the result.
    Check if drive is less than 0.
    If that condition is true, then perform the following actions.
        Set drive to 0.
    End the conditional block.
    Calculate the product of 0.02 and drive.
    Calculate the sum of adaptation and the result.
    Set adaptation to the result.
    Calculate the product of 6.0 and curvature.
    Calculate the sum of drive and the result.
    Display the result to the console.
    Set older_depth to previous_depth.
    Set previous_depth to centre_depth.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

# Python: the SA1 computation is a short spatial filter plus a slow leak, and
# Python expresses both without any library at all.
THRESHOLD = 10.0    # micrometres of indentation before firing begins
STATIC_GAIN = 1.0   # spikes/s per micrometre of sustained indentation
EDGE_GAIN = 6.0     # spikes/s per micrometre of surface curvature
ADAPT_RATE = 0.02   # fraction of drive folded into adaptation per sample
MAX_RATE = 200.0    # ceiling set by the refractory period

def sa1_scan(profile):
    """profile: indentation depth in micrometres at successive scan points."""
    rates, adaptation = [], 0.0
    for i in range(1, len(profile) - 1):
        # Second spatial difference: positive values mark the edge of a dot.
        curvature = max(0.0, profile[i - 1] + profile[i + 1] - 2.0 * profile[i])
        pressed = max(0.0, profile[i] - THRESHOLD)
        drive = max(0.0, pressed - adaptation)   # slowly adapting plateau
        adaptation += ADAPT_RATE * drive
        rates.append(min(MAX_RATE, STATIC_GAIN * drive + EDGE_GAIN * curvature))
    return rates

if __name__ == "__main__":
    # A flat plateau with a single raised braille dot in the middle.
    surface = [0.0] * 5 + [60.0] * 10 + [140.0] * 3 + [60.0] * 10 + [0.0] * 5
    out = sa1_scan(surface)
    print("rate on the flat plateau:", round(out[8], 1), "spikes/s")
    print("rate at the dot edge:   ", round(max(out), 1), "spikes/s")

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

It is the only touch afferent whose transducer is a separate cell of skin origin that synapses onto it with serotonin.

A large myelinated fibre that fires irregularly and without pause under a steady press, answers a raised dot far more strongly than a flat plateau of the same depth, and ends on ATOH1 dependent Merkel cells inside the epidermis, is an SA1 and nothing else.

CHAPTER 241. A-BETA SLOWLY ADAPTING TYPE 2 (SA2) (Skin-Stretch Posture Neuron) (NON-COG)

This is the cell that knows your hand is open when your hand is touching nothing, because it reads the stretch of your own skin the way a sail reads wind.

SA2 is a series number that says nothing except that it adapts slowly and was catalogued second.

Skin-Stretch names the stimulus precisely, because these endings are excited by pulling the skin along the long axis of the receptor rather than by pressing into it, and they cluster at the finger and wrist joints and along the palm folds while being scarce in the fingertips.

Posture names the faculty, since human microneurography shows these afferents track voluntary limb and hand movement well enough to signal joint angle, giving the skin itself a share in position sense.

Neuron marks it as an afferent recording a genuine second sense of self carried by a touch fibre.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

An A beta slowly adapting type 2 afferent (SA2) is a fast myelinated dorsal root ganglion (DRG) neuron that answers skin being pulled sideways rather than pressed inwards.

Slowly adapting means it holds its discharge for the whole stimulus, and type 2 marks it out from SA1 by a regular rather than a ragged rhythm.

It measures tension in the skin sheet, turning the hand into a strain gauge that reports finger posture and the direction of grip force.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Pseudounipolar, a round dendrite free cell body with a T shaped branch point.

The ending is traditionally a Ruffini corpuscle, a spindle shaped capsule about 1.4 millimetres long in the deep dermis, in which the fibre braids among collagen, so pulling the collagen taut squeezes the fibre like a Chinese finger trap.

Honesty is required, because careful searches find remarkably few Ruffini corpuscles in the human index finger pad even though SA2 recordings there are routine, so the human end organ is unsettled.

SIZE:

Median cell body diameter is 90 to 100 micrometres and the fibre is 6 to 12 micrometres thick with myelin.

The receptive field is large and poorly bounded, about five times the area of an SA1 field on the same skin, often 50 square millimetres or more with no sharp border at all.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

• Cutaneous units in glabrous skin, densest around the nail, encoding fingertip force direction.

• Cutaneous units in hairy skin, spread far more evenly than SA1 units.

• Joint capsule and ligament Ruffini endings, reporting joint angle near the ends of the range rather than in the middle.

• Periodontal ligament endings around tooth roots, reporting bite force and direction, with cell bodies in the trigeminal ganglion.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Cell bodies sit in dorsal root ganglia at all spinal levels.

Endings sit in the deep dermis and subcutaneous layer, at the interface between dermis and the fibrous tissue beneath, where skin tension is greatest, concentrating in the nail bed, the finger folds, joint capsules and ligaments.

Central branches enter through the dorsal root entry zone and terminate in laminae III to V and in the gracile and cuneate nuclei.

NUMBER:

Take the whole body figure of about 230,000 tactile afferents, remove the roughly 19 percent that are facial, and apply the near one fifth share SA2 units hold in hand recordings.

That gives about 35,000 SA2 neurons, plausible range 20,000 to 55,000, which is 0.35 percent of the roughly 10 million DRG neurons and about 0.00004 percent of all neurons.

It falls inside the LTMR family total rather than adding to it, at low to moderate confidence, because SA2 units are systematically under sampled by microelectrode recording.

Johansson and Vallbo found that unlike SA1 and RA1 units they spread evenly from palm to fingertip, so the body total scales with skin area rather than with dexterity.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Resting voltage near minus 60 millivolts with a narrow action potential.

The signature is a regular, metronomic discharge, and most units carry an ongoing resting rate of 5 to 20 spikes per second even when nothing touches the skin, because resting skin is never fully slack.

They are about six times less sensitive to indentation than SA1 units but two to four times more sensitive to lateral stretch, conducting at 35 to 75 metres per second.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Central terminals release glutamate in laminae III to V and in the dorsal column nuclei.

Because SA2 signals resemble joint angle information, their output converges in the cuneate nucleus and cortex with true proprioceptor traffic, and the brain fuses the two into one estimate of posture.

Presynaptic GABA-A receptors let the cord suppress this steady background during movement, which is why you are not constantly aware of your own skin tension.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Fast and myelinated, with the last heminode just outside the capsule serving as the spike initiation site.

Branching in the skin is sparse, and the huge vague field comes from collagen carrying strain over long distances rather than from axonal spread.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Glutamate, packaged by VGLUT1, with no substance P and no calcitonin gene related peptide.

PIEZO2 is required for the stretch response, and PIEZO2 loss of function patients lose exactly the skin stretch cues these cells provide.

MYELINATION:

Myelinated in the A beta range by Schwann cells peripherally and oligodendrocytes centrally, with internodes near one millimetre.

Myelin stops at the capsule wall, leaving the braided fibre inside naked so collagen presses directly on mechanosensitive membrane.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Continuously moderate rather than peaky, which matters, because an SA2 unit is one of the few sensory neurons that never fully stops.

Holding a background discharge for a lifetime is a real energy cost, met by dense mitochondria at the terminal and the nodes.

PLASTICITY:

Sensitivity depends on the mechanical state of the surrounding collagen, and collagen stiffens with age and sun damage, so gain drifts across a lifetime.

Habitually stretched skin, as on the palms of manual workers, remodels its dermis and changes the signal sent.

After nerve injury the ongoing discharge can become unremitting, contributing to a sense of a tight band when nothing is swollen.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Satellite glial cells wrap the cell body as for every DRG neuron, with nothing unusual there.

The special support is structural rather than glial, because fibroblasts, the collagen making cells of the dermis, build and continuously remodel the strands that are this receptor's working parts.

The capsule cells are perineurial, the flattened barrier cells that normally sheathe a nerve bundle, so the receptor is a pocket of nerve sheath turned inside out.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove SA2 and the hand loses the sense of its own shape.

You would still feel contact, texture and vibration, but not which way a held object was pulling, nor the direction of the force you were applying to it.

Skin stretch cues alone let people judge finger position, and single unit microstimulation published in 2022 showed that driving one SA2 unit produces a conscious sensation, so its loss removes a real feeling.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

The computation is largely mechanical and happens outside the neuron.

Collagen strands act as a directional filter, so each unit is tuned to one direction of pull, firing hardest along its best axis and least across it, roughly following a cosine.

Collagen is viscoelastic, creeping under load like slowly stretching toffee, so a held stretch produces a discharge that sags over tens of seconds, and because the cell has a resting rate it can also signal by falling silent.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: lateral skin strain, dimensionless, useful range 0 to 0.30; strain direction in degrees, 0 to 360; perpendicular indentation in micrometres, weakly effective; joint angle in degrees for capsular units.

• OUTPUTS: glutamate in laminae III to V and in the gracile and cuneate nuclei, as a regular train of 0 to about 100 spikes per second with a non zero floor.

• STATE: membrane voltage, viscoelastic creep variable, adaptation level, current resting rate.

• PARAMETERS: resting rate 5 to 20 spikes per second, strain gain about 200 spikes per second per unit strain, one preferred direction per cell, indentation sensitivity about one sixth of SA1.

• TIME CONSTANTS: transduction under 1 millisecond, fast adaptation 20 to 50 milliseconds, viscoelastic creep 10 to 60 seconds, collagen remodelling weeks.

• FAILURE MODE: PIEZO2 loss of function removes the stretch signal and degrades felt hand posture; nerve injury turns the ongoing discharge into phantom tightness; ageing and diabetic stiffening of dermal collagen distort the gain so force direction is misjudged, one cause of dropped objects in later life.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to convert skin strain and its direction into the regular, direction tuned discharge of an SA2 afferent.
Create a number variable called creep_state with initial value 0.0.
Create a number variable called preferred_direction with initial value 45.0.
For each sample in strain_recording, perform the following operations.
    Access the item at position 1 in sample.
    Set strain_size to the result.
    Access the item at position 2 in sample.
    Set strain_direction to the result.
    Calculate the difference of strain_direction and preferred_direction.
    Set direction_factor to the cosine of the result.
    Check if direction_factor is less than 0.
    If that condition is true, then perform the following actions.
        Set direction_factor to 0.
    End the conditional block.
    Calculate the product of strain_size and direction_factor.
    Calculate the difference of the result and creep_state.
    Set drive to the result.
    Check if drive is less than 0.
    If that condition is true, then perform the following actions.
        Set drive to 0.
    End the conditional block.
    Calculate the product of 0.004 and drive.
    Calculate the sum of creep_state and the result.
    Set creep_state to the result.
    Calculate the product of 200.0 and drive.
    Calculate the sum of 12.0 and the result.
    Set firing_rate to the result.
    Display firing_rate to the console.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

# Python: the SA2 model needs a cosine, a leak and a floor, and the math module
# supplies the first while the rest is plain arithmetic.
import math

RESTING_RATE = 12.0      # spikes/s with no stimulus at all
STRAIN_GAIN = 200.0      # spikes/s per unit of effective strain
PREFERRED_DEG = 45.0     # this cell's best direction of pull
CREEP_RATE = 0.004       # collagen creep folded in per millisecond sample
MAX_RATE = 100.0         # ceiling for a regular slowly adapting unit

def sa2(strain_series):
    """strain_series: list of (strain magnitude, pull direction in degrees)."""
    rates, creep = [], 0.0
    for magnitude, direction in strain_series:
        # Directional tuning: half wave rectified cosine about the best axis.
        tuning = max(0.0, math.cos(math.radians(direction - PREFERRED_DEG)))
        drive = max(0.0, magnitude * tuning - creep)   # viscoelastic sag
        creep += CREEP_RATE * drive
        rates.append(min(MAX_RATE, RESTING_RATE + STRAIN_GAIN * drive))
    return rates

if __name__ == "__main__":
    aligned = [(0.10, 45.0)] * 2000        # 2 s pull along the best axis
    across = [(0.10, 135.0)] * 2000        # same pull, wrong axis
    print("aligned, first sample:", round(sa2(aligned)[0], 1), "spikes/s")
    print("aligned, after 2 s:  ", round(sa2(aligned)[-1], 1), "spikes/s")
    print("orthogonal pull:     ", round(sa2(across)[0], 1), "spikes/s")

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

It is the only cutaneous afferent already talking before you touch it, and the only one reporting the direction of a pull rather than the fact of a press.

A large myelinated unit with a regular resting rhythm, an enormous fuzzy edged field, far less sensitivity to indentation than its SA1 neighbour but more to stretch, and a discharge that sags over half a minute, can be nothing else.

CHAPTER 242. A-BETA RAPIDLY ADAPTING TYPE 1 (RA1) (Slip-Catching Flutter Neuron) (NON-COG)

Every time a glass begins to slip from your fingers, this cell catches it about seventy milliseconds before you know anything has happened.

RA1 is a barcode for a fast-decaying response, not a description of purpose.

Slip-Catching names the job that matters most in daily life, because a single one of these afferents gathers signal from thirty to eighty Meissner corpuscles and fires the instant a held object begins to move against the skin, triggering the reflexive tightening of grip.

Flutter names its frequency home, since it responds best to low-frequency skin motion in roughly the twenty to forty hertz band that people perceive as flutter.

Neuron marks it as an afferent that exists to notice movement across the skin rather than steady contact.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

An A beta rapidly adapting type 1 afferent (RA1), also called a fast adapting type 1 or FA1 unit, is a fast myelinated dorsal root ganglion (DRG) neuron ending inside a Meissner corpuscle.

Rapidly adapting means it fires a burst when a stimulus begins and another when it ends, and is silent in between, so it reports change rather than state.

Its subject is motion across the skin, and that is why gripping works, since gripping is not about squeezing hard but about catching the first microscopic slip.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Pseudounipolar, with a round dendrite free cell body and a T shaped branch point.

Each twig enters a Meissner corpuscle, an egg shaped body roughly 40 by 100 micrometres sitting in a dermal papilla, one of the upward bumps of dermis under a fingerprint ridge.

Inside, the naked fibre zigzags between a stack of flattened lamellar cells like a ribbon threaded through a pile of pancakes, and collagen anchors the corpuscle to the epidermis above.

SIZE:

Median cell body diameter is 90 to 100 micrometres and axon diameter 6 to 12 micrometres with myelin.

The fingertip receptive field averages about 25 square millimetres, larger than an SA1 field but still sharply bounded.

Reported Meissner densities vary widely with method, from about 3 per square millimetre in one histological count and about 12 by living confocal microscopy of the little finger, up to about 50 at the very tip of the index finger.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

• Glabrous units of fingertip, palm and sole, with the highest density and smallest fields.

• Lip, tongue and nipple units, where Meissner like corpuscles are abundant, the facial ones having trigeminal cell bodies.

• Hair follicle rapidly adapting units, filling the same niche in hairy skin using lanceolate endings, palisades of bare membrane running alongside the hair shaft.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Cell bodies occupy dorsal root ganglia at every spinal level, with the face served by the trigeminal ganglion.

Endings occupy the dermal papillae of glabrous skin, at the interface between dermis and epidermis, only about 0.5 millimetres below the surface, closer to the outside world than any other encapsulated receptor.

Central branches terminate in laminae III to V and ascend the dorsal columns unbroken to the gracile nucleus for the foot and the cuneate nucleus for the hand, crossing the dorsal root entry zone.

NUMBER:

Meissner corpuscles exist only in glabrous skin, which bounds the count tightly.

The glabrous skin of one young adult hand holds about 17,000 tactile afferents, of which RA1 units are the largest single class, and each foot sole holds about 4,000 in total.

Adding lips, soles and genital skin and excluding the roughly 19 percent of body tactile afferents that are facial gives about 45,000 RA1 neurons, plausible range 25,000 to 70,000.

That is 0.45 percent of the roughly 10 million DRG neurons and about 0.00005 percent of all neurons, at moderate confidence, with density reported near 141 units per square centimetre at the distal fingertip.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Resting voltage near minus 60 millivolts, with a narrow spike and a very high safety factor, so almost every impulse that starts arrives.

A sustained press gives one or two spikes at onset, silence during the hold, and one or two at release.

To vibration these cells entrain, firing one spike per cycle over roughly 5 to 50 hertz with best sensitivity at 20 to 40 hertz, the range felt as flutter, from a threshold of a few micrometres, conducting at 35 to 75 metres per second.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

The peripheral arrangement is many to many: one corpuscle receives several afferents and one afferent supplies several corpuscles, which is why RA1 fields exceed SA1 fields despite the smaller receptor.

Centrally, glutamate is released in laminae III to V and in the cuneate and gracile nuclei.

The signal drives a loop that raises grip force automatically 70 to 100 milliseconds after a slip begins, long before the event reaches awareness.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Fast, myelinated and branching profusely in the dermis, with myelin shed at a heminode just before each corpuscle.

Each corpuscle can start spikes independently and the axon sums them, so one cell reads a small array of motion detectors at once.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Glutamate, packaged by VGLUT1, with no substance P and no calcitonin gene related peptide.

Many RA1 cells carry parvalbumin, the same protein used to label proprioceptors, so parvalbumin alone does not identify a proprioceptor.

Development and corpuscle formation depend on brain derived neurotrophic factor through the receptor TrkB, also written NTRK2, and on the receptor RET.

MYELINATION:

Myelinated in the A beta range with internodes near one millimetre, by Schwann cells peripherally and oligodendrocytes centrally.

The corpuscle's lamellar cells are themselves Schwann cell lineage, so the receptor is a Schwann cell that abandoned insulation for mechanics, and end organ formation depends on axon to glia signalling through molecules including Netrin-G1.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Bursty rather than continuous, and cheaper per second than an SA1 unit because the cell is silent whenever nothing moves.

Peak cost is high, since a scanning fingertip drives these cells at hundreds of spikes per second, and the corpuscle is a living structure the axon must continuously maintain.

PLASTICITY:

Meissner corpuscle density falls markedly with age, tracking the measurable decline in tactile spatial acuity across adult life.

Corpuscles are lost early in diabetic and other sensory neuropathies, and living confocal microscopy of Meissner corpuscles is now used clinically as a measure of nerve fibre loss.

Denervated corpuscles shrink within weeks and reinnervation rebuilds them, so the structure is under continuous neural control.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Satellite glial cells jacket the cell body in the standard way.

The distinctive support is peripheral, because the lamellar cells are modified Schwann cells that stopped making myelin and instead form a stack of mechanically coupled plates, so a glial cell has become the working part of the sense organ.

Collagen fibrils tether the corpuscle to the basal epidermis, so keratinocytes act as the first stage of its mechanics, and denervated skin makes these glia lose their identity.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove RA1 and the hand becomes clumsy in a very particular way.

Static touch survives, so a held object still feels solid and shaped, but the sense of motion across the skin would go, so you would not feel a surface starting to slide and would grip everything far too hard or drop it.

Fine flutter would vanish, fabric drawn between finger and thumb would feel flat, and the reflex that keeps a full cup steady while you walk would not fire.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

The corpuscle is a mechanical differentiator, passing on the rate of change of pressure and discarding steady pressure before any ion channel is involved.

PIEZO2 then inactivates within about 5 to 15 milliseconds, so the neuron adds a molecular stage of change detection on top of the mechanical one.

Fingerprint ridges spaced 0.4 to 0.5 millimetres apart turn a scanning movement into vibration in exactly this cell's preferred band, so the fingerprint is a tuned antenna, and shared corpuscles correlate nearby afferents in a way the brain exploits to localise contact.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: indentation velocity in micrometres per second, the effective drive; vibration amplitude in micrometres and frequency in hertz, useful range 2 to 100; contact onset and offset; slip velocity in millimetres per second.

• OUTPUTS: glutamate in laminae III to V and in the gracile and cuneate nuclei, 0 to about 250 spikes per second, phase locked to vibration where present.

• STATE: membrane voltage, PIEZO2 inactivation level, corpuscle filter state, phase accumulator.

• PARAMETERS: best frequency 20 to 40 hertz, threshold 2 to 10 micrometres at best frequency, receptive field about 25 square millimetres, conduction 35 to 75 metres per second, high pass corner near 3 hertz.

• TIME CONSTANTS: PIEZO2 activation under 1 millisecond, PIEZO2 inactivation 5 to 15 milliseconds, corpuscle relaxation 10 to 30 milliseconds, grip reflex latency 70 to 100 milliseconds.

• FAILURE MODE: PIEZO2 loss of function abolishes the response and leaves patients unable to grade grip without vision; carpal tunnel compression blocks these fibres early, giving tingling and clumsiness; diabetic neuropathy destroys Meissner corpuscles before nerve conduction studies turn abnormal.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to convert a skin indentation trace into the onset, offset and flutter locked discharge of an RA1 afferent.
Create a number variable called previous_depth with initial value 0.0.
Create a number variable called inactivation with initial value 0.0.
For each current_depth in indentation_recording, perform the following operations.
    Calculate the difference of current_depth and previous_depth.
    Set depth_change to the result.
    Set previous_depth to current_depth.
    Check if depth_change is less than 0.
    If that condition is true, then perform the following actions.
        Calculate the product of depth_change and -1.
        Set depth_change to the result.
    End the conditional block.
    Calculate the quotient of depth_change divided by 0.001.
    Set indentation_speed to the result.
    Calculate the difference of 1.0 and inactivation.
    Set available_channels to the result.
    Calculate the product of indentation_speed and available_channels.
    Calculate the product of the result and 0.35.
    Set firing_rate to the result.
    Calculate the product of 0.90 and inactivation.
    Set inactivation to the result.
    Check if indentation_speed is greater than 0.
    If that condition is true, then perform the following actions.
        Calculate the product of 0.10 and available_channels.
        Calculate the sum of inactivation and the result.
        Set inactivation to the result.
    End the conditional block.
    Display firing_rate to the console.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

# Python: the RA1 model is a differentiator plus a channel inactivation term,
# and Python keeps both visible in a dozen readable lines.
DT = 0.001              # sample interval in seconds
VELOCITY_GAIN = 0.35    # spikes/s per micrometre per second of skin movement
INACT_RATE = 0.10       # PIEZO2 inactivation accumulated per active sample
RECOVERY_RATE = 0.10    # inactivation removed per quiet sample
MAX_RATE = 250.0        # refractory ceiling

def ra1(depths):
    """depths: skin indentation in micrometres, one value per millisecond."""
    rates, previous, inactivation = [], depths[0], 0.0
    for depth in depths:
        speed = abs(depth - previous) / DT   # the corpuscle differentiates
        previous = depth
        available = 1.0 - inactivation       # fraction of PIEZO2 still usable
        rates.append(min(MAX_RATE, VELOCITY_GAIN * speed * available))
        inactivation *= 1.0 - RECOVERY_RATE  # channels recover every step
        if speed > 0.0:
            inactivation += INACT_RATE * available
    return rates

if __name__ == "__main__":
    hold = [0.0] * 20 + [200.0] * 300 + [0.0] * 20   # step on, hold, step off
    out = ra1(hold)
    print("onset burst: ", round(out[20], 1), "spikes/s")
    print("during hold: ", round(out[200], 1), "spikes/s")
    print("offset burst:", round(out[320], 1), "spikes/s")

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

It is the touch afferent that answers twice and never in between: once when you arrive and once when you leave.

A large myelinated fibre with a sharply bounded field of about 25 square millimetres, entraining to a 30 hertz flutter, silent under a steady press, and ending in Schwann derived lamellae in a dermal papilla beneath a fingerprint ridge, is an RA1 and nothing else.

CHAPTER 243. A-BETA RAPIDLY ADAPTING TYPE 2 (RA2) (Whole-Hand Vibration Neuron) (NON-COG)

Deep under your palm sit a few hundred structures the size of a grain of rice, each wired to one nerve fibre, and together they detect a ripple ten nanometres high.

RA2 is a catalogue entry, and its partner structure the Pacinian corpuscle is an eponym-shaped anatomical term that also hides the function.

Whole-Hand names its extraordinary spatial reach, because a single Pacinian afferent can be driven by vibration entering anywhere on the hand and even by vibration conducted through bone from a distant part of the limb, so it reports the state of a whole structure rather than a point.

Vibration names its stimulus, and it is the most sensitive vibration detector in the body, with peak sensitivity near two hundred and fifty hertz and useful responses across roughly fifty to five hundred hertz.

Neuron marks it as the cell that lets a person feel the texture of a surface through the tip of a held tool.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

An A beta rapidly adapting type 2 afferent (RA2), also called a fast adapting type 2 or Pacinian unit, is a fast myelinated dorsal root ganglion (DRG) neuron ending inside a Pacinian corpuscle.

A Pacinian corpuscle is the largest sensory receptor in the body, an onion of up to about sixty fluid separated lamellae, half a millimetre to four millimetres long, just visible to the naked eye.

Its subject is high frequency vibration, and it is the most sensitive mechanical detector in the human body, which is why you feel a phone buzzing through a pocket and the moment a chisel bites wood.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Pseudounipolar, with a round dendrite free cell body and a T shaped branch point.

The peripheral fibre runs to a single corpuscle, loses its myelin at the entrance, and continues as a straight unbranched rod up the central core, surrounded by concentric lamellae separated by fluid.

The assembly behaves like a shock absorber: squeeze it slowly and the fluid redistributes so the core feels nothing, but hit it fast and the fluid cannot move and the core deforms.

SIZE:

Median cell body diameter is 90 to 100 micrometres and axon diameter 6 to 12 micrometres with myelin.

The corpuscle is by far the largest receptor in the body at up to four millimetres, thousands of times the volume of a Meissner corpuscle.

The receptive field is enormous and has no useful border, often covering a whole finger, a whole hand, or much of a limb, because vibration travels through tissue.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

There is essentially one functional type, which is remarkable in a family defined by diversity, and variation is by location rather than by kind.

• Cutaneous and subcutaneous corpuscles of the hand and foot, the classic RA2 units.

• Interosseous corpuscles along the membrane between the forearm and lower leg bones, picking up vibration through the skeleton.

• Periosteal corpuscles on the outer surface of bone, and corpuscles in the mesentery, pancreas, joint capsules and external genitalia.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Cell bodies sit in dorsal root ganglia at all spinal levels.

Corpuscles lie in the deep dermis, subcutaneous fat, interosseous membranes, periosteum, joint capsules, the mesentery that suspends the intestines, and the genitalia.

In the hand they cluster around the digital nerves rather than at the skin surface, so this is a deep receptor reporting on surface events.

Central branches terminate in laminae III to V and ascend the dorsal columns to the gracile and cuneate nuclei, crossing the dorsal root entry zone.

NUMBER:

A cadaver study found a mean of 300 Pacinian corpuscles per hand, range 192 to 424, with 44 to 60 percent in the fingers, 23 to 48 percent around the knuckles and 8 to 18 percent in the thenar and hypothenar pads.

Since one afferent serves one corpuscle, those are afferent counts too, and extending to feet, interosseous membranes, periosteum, mesentery and genitalia gives about 10,000 RA2 neurons, plausible range 5,000 to 25,000.

That is 0.1 percent of the roughly 10 million DRG neurons and about 0.00001 percent of all neurons, making this by far the rarest of the four A beta classes.

Confidence is low, because microelectrode recording attributes over a tenth of hand tactile units to Pacinian afferents, which would imply thousands in the hand alone, and the likely resolution is that skin dissection misses corpuscles lying deep along nerve trunks.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Resting voltage near minus 60 millivolts with a narrow, fast spike.

A steady press produces exactly one spike at onset and one at release and nothing in between, the most extreme adaptation of any receptor in the body.

The cell entrains to vibration from roughly 20 to 1000 hertz with a best frequency near 250 hertz, where the threshold is about ten nanometres of skin movement, and it conducts at 35 to 75 metres per second.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Peripherally the arrangement is strictly one afferent to one corpuscle, found nowhere else in the touch system.

Centrally the terminal releases glutamate in laminae III to V and in the gracile and cuneate nuclei.

Because the fields are so large, spatial information is discarded and the population instead codes frequency and intensity, read out in cortex as texture and as events at a tool tip.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Fast, myelinated and utterly unbranched at its peripheral end, so one axon equals one receptor.

The first node of Ranvier lies just inside the corpuscle, so the receptor physically encloses the spike initiation site, and the T junction limits sustained output when a 700 hertz vibration would otherwise demand 700 spikes per second.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Glutamate, packaged by VGLUT1, with no substance P and no calcitonin gene related peptide.

PIEZO2 is the transduction channel, and human PIEZO2 loss of function abolishes vibration sense.

MYELINATION:

Myelinated in the A beta range peripherally by Schwann cells and centrally by oligodendrocytes, with internodes near one millimetre.

Myelin is stripped at the corpuscle entrance and the inner core lamellae take over as the axon's covering, and those lamellae are Schwann cell derived, so once again a glial cell has become the sense organ.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Low at rest and enormous in bursts, since a hand on a vibrating tool drives these cells at hundreds of spikes per second for as long as the tool runs.

Maintaining a receptor several millimetres long with tens of concentric layers is a substantial burden carried by one axon.

PLASTICITY:

The corpuscle population declines dramatically across life, one of the best documented losses in the somatosensory system.

Serial sections show roughly 80 to 180 corpuscles in the end segment of a fetal digit against fewer than 40 in an elderly one, with the fetal tree like arrangements rarely found in old age.

Occasionally the opposite happens, since repeated trauma can provoke Pacinian corpuscle hyperplasia, a painful overgrowth in the hand.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Satellite glial cells envelope the cell body in the usual way.

Peripherally the support system is the receptor, because the inner core lamellae are modified Schwann cells and the outer capsule is built of perineurial cells, the flattened barrier cells that normally sheathe a nerve bundle.

Between the lamellae sits an actively maintained fluid providing the viscous damping, so the glia are not holding the axon but filtering for it, and a denervated corpuscle collapses within weeks.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Delete RA2 and the body loses the ability to feel through objects.

Static touch, edges, slip and stretch all survive, so a held object still feels solid, but fine texture would flatten, because fine texture is coded by the vibrations a moving finger generates.

Tools would become dead sticks, with no feel of the pen tip on paper and no feel of the scalpel meeting tissue, which is precisely the deficit of hand arm vibration syndrome.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

The most instructive experiment in sensory biology was performed on this cell: peel the lamellae away one by one and the naked ending stops being rapidly adapting and answers steady pressure like a slowly adapting unit.

That proves the adaptation is mechanical, performed by the capsule, and not a property of the neuron at all, so most of this cell's computation is done by a structure it merely lives inside.

Because vibration propagates through tissue over many centimetres, the population performs a distributed, whole hand frequency analysis, with corpuscles at different depths receiving differently filtered copies of one event.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: vibration amplitude in micrometres from 0.01 upward; vibration frequency in hertz, useful range 20 to 1000; contact transients; tissue borne vibration arriving from centimetres away.

• OUTPUTS: glutamate in laminae III to V and in the gracile and cuneate nuclei, phase locked, 0 to about 500 spikes per second, saturating at one spike per cycle.

• STATE: membrane voltage, capsule filter state of at least second order, refractory timer, phase accumulator.

• PARAMETERS: best frequency 200 to 300 hertz, threshold at best frequency about 0.01 micrometres, high pass corner about 30 to 60 hertz, receptive field effectively a whole hand, exactly one afferent per corpuscle.

• TIME CONSTANTS: capsule relaxation 1 to 5 milliseconds, transduction under 1 millisecond, refractory period 1 to 2 milliseconds which sets the entrainment ceiling, corpuscle loss over decades.

• FAILURE MODE: PIEZO2 loss of function removes vibration sense entirely; diabetic and chemotherapy induced neuropathy destroy these afferents early, which is why tuning fork testing at the toe is the standard screening test; chronic vibrating tool use produces hand arm vibration syndrome with numbness and blanching fingers.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to convert a vibration trace into the phase locked discharge of a Pacinian RA2 afferent.
Create a number variable called previous_input with initial value 0.0.
Create a number variable called filtered_value with initial value 0.0.
For each current_input in vibration_recording, perform the following operations.
    Calculate the difference of current_input and previous_input.
    Calculate the sum of filtered_value and the result.
    Calculate the product of the result and 0.97.
    Set filtered_value to the result.
    Set previous_input to current_input.
    Set rectified_value to filtered_value.
    Check if rectified_value is less than 0.
    If that condition is true, then perform the following actions.
        Calculate the product of rectified_value and -1.
        Set rectified_value to the result.
    End the conditional block.
    Calculate the difference of rectified_value and 0.01.
    Set drive to the result.
    Check if drive is less than 0.
    If that condition is true, then perform the following actions.
        Set drive to 0.
    End the conditional block.
    Calculate the product of 900.0 and drive.
    Set firing_rate to the result.
    Check if firing_rate is greater than 500.
    If that condition is true, then perform the following actions.
        Set firing_rate to 500.
    End the conditional block.
    Display firing_rate to the console.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

# Python: a one pole high pass filter plus a threshold reproduces the Pacinian
# capsule and its channel, and the math module supplies the test tone.
import math

DT = 0.0001            # 100 microsecond steps, needed above 250 Hz
ALPHA = 0.97           # capsule high pass coefficient, sets the corner
THRESHOLD = 0.01       # micrometres of core deformation before firing
GAIN = 900.0           # spikes/s per micrometre of suprathreshold deformation
MAX_RATE = 500.0       # entrainment ceiling from the refractory period

def pacinian(trace):
    """trace: skin displacement in micrometres sampled every DT seconds."""
    rates, previous, filtered = [], trace[0], 0.0
    for value in trace:
        # Discrete one pole high pass: steady pressure is discarded.
        filtered = ALPHA * (filtered + value - previous)
        previous = value
        rates.append(min(MAX_RATE, GAIN * max(0.0, abs(filtered) - THRESHOLD)))
    return rates

def tone(frequency, amplitude, seconds):
    n = int(seconds / DT)
    return [amplitude * math.sin(2.0 * math.pi * frequency * i * DT)
            for i in range(n)]

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("250 Hz, 0.05 um:", round(max(pacinian(tone(250, 0.05, 0.05))), 1))
    print("  5 Hz, 0.05 um:", round(max(pacinian(tone(5, 0.05, 0.5))), 1))
    print("steady 50 um press:", round(max(pacinian([50.0] * 500)), 1))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

It is the only receptor in the human body with a strict one to one relationship between sense organ and nerve fibre, and the only one whose adaptation can be removed by peeling the receptor.

A single unbranched axon running to a rice grain sized onion of sixty lamellae, firing one spike at the start and one at the end of a press, entraining to 250 hertz at ten nanometres, with a field covering an entire hand, can be nothing else in biology.

CHAPTER 244. A-DELTA LOW-THRESHOLD MECHANORECEPTORS, ALSO CALLED D-HAIR CELLS (Faint-Breeze Hair Neuron) (NON-COG)

Blow gently across your forearm and this cell answers first, the most touch sensitive neuron you own.

A-delta names only axon calibre and D-hair names only a hair subtype, so neither says what the cell achieves.

Faint-Breeze names its distinction, because these are the most sensitive mechanoreceptors in skin, responding to displacements smaller than any other class can register, which is why a moving air current or a settling insect is felt at all.

Hair names the machinery, since the endings wrap around and run lengthwise along hair follicles and are deflected by the lever action of the shaft, and their receptive fields are unusually large so a single cell watches a wide patch of skin.

Neuron marks it as an afferent teaching that hairs are not decoration but the amplifying antennae of a warning-grade touch channel.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

It is a sensory nerve cell that reports the faintest bending of a body hair, and it holds the lowest mechanical threshold ever measured in mammalian skin.

Low-threshold means gentle force rather than damage, A-delta names its middling speed, and it fires only while a hair moves, hardest when the stroke runs against the lie of the hair.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

The cell body is pseudounipolar: one stalk leaves a round soma and forks like a T, one arm to skin and one to spinal cord, with no dendrites.

In skin it lays spear-shaped lanceolate fingers along a hair follicle as a half-collar, each finger clasped by terminal Schwann cells, and that lopsided collar creates direction selectivity.

SIZE:

The soma is 30 to 45 micrometres across, a micrometre being one thousandth of a millimetre, the axon is 1 to 5 micrometres wide, and one cell holds about 31 hair follicles in mouse.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

Subtypes divide by hair type, zigzag hairs at some 74 per cent and awl or auchene hairs at some 25 per cent in mouse, and by preferred stroke direction, while the two human A-delta clusters found by sequencing remain unnamed.

WHERE IT LIVES:

The soma sits in a dorsal root ganglion beside every vertebra from neck to sacrum, the border post where the peripheral nervous system meets the central nervous system.

Its endings occupy hairy skin only, its axon crosses the dorsal root entry zone where Schwann cell insulation hands over to oligodendrocyte insulation, and its central arbors fill inner lamina II and lamina III of the dorsal horn.

NUMBER:

A defensible estimate is 100,000 to 300,000 in a whole adult, mid-point near 150,000, or 1 to 3 per cent of the roughly 10 million human dorsal root ganglion neurons.

Confidence is low because the figure is scaled from rodents, and against about 86 billion neurons overall it is some two cells per million.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Resting voltage is near minus 60 millivolts and the spike is narrow, lacking the shoulder of pain fibres.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Nothing synapses on the cell body; in the cord it excites protein kinase C gamma interneurons and vertical cells, and its terminals receive axoaxonic gamma-aminobutyric acid contacts, the machinery of pain gating.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Conduction runs at 5 to 30 metres per second, D-hair units near 5 to 15, along up to a metre of cable whose T-junction filters out the fastest bursts.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

It releases glutamate, the main excitatory messenger, through vesicular glutamate transporter 1, carries no calcitonin gene-related peptide or substance P, and bears TrkB, the receptor for brain-derived neurotrophic factor.

MYELINATION:

Myelin, the fatty insulation that speeds conduction, is thin here, a few wraps from Schwann cells outside the cord and oligodendrocytes inside, and it stops before the follicle so bare membrane can feel the hair.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Most energy goes on the sodium potassium pump along a metre of axon, firing is cheap because the cell rests when hairs are still, and fenestrated capillaries feed the ganglion straight from blood.

PLASTICITY:

In adults neurotrophin-4 acting on TrkB raises the Cav3.2 current, and each hair growth cycle forces the ending to be rebuilt.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

The special partner is the terminal Schwann cell, whose grip on each lanceolate finger is part of the force sensor itself.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove it and hairy skin keeps pressure, texture and pain but loses its trip-wire: no mosquito landing, no spider walking, no felt direction of a draught.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

Direction selectivity is solved by anatomy, in the half-collar, before any electrical processing happens.

Beyond that, the T-junction filters spike trains, Cav3.2 amplifies movement below the noise floor of most neurons, terminal Schwann cells share transduction, cooling shifts responsiveness so temperature becomes an input to a touch cell, presynaptic inhibition lets the cord mute the cell, and local protein synthesis maintains the axon a metre from its nucleus.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: hair tip speed per follicle in micrometres per millisecond, signed plus one against the grain and minus one with it, from 20 to 40 follicles; presynaptic inhibition from 0 to 1.

• OUTPUTS: spikes releasing glutamate onto protein kinase C gamma interneurons and vertical cells of inner lamina II and lamina III.

• STATE: membrane voltage; Piezo2 open fraction; Kv1.2 inactivation; Cav3.2 availability; adaptation accumulator; time since last spike.

• PARAMETERS: threshold below 0.1 millinewtons; conduction 5 to 15 metres per second; about 31 follicles per field; direction gain ratio near 4 to 1.

• TIME CONSTANTS: transduction under 1 millisecond; adaptation 10 to 30 milliseconds; refractory 2 milliseconds; foot to cord delay 10 to 100 milliseconds.

• FAILURE MODE: endings retract in small fibre neuropathy and light touch dulls; after nerve injury these terminals help drive allodynia, pain from a gentle stroke.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to turn hair movement in one receptive field into the direction selective, rapidly adapting burst of an A-delta low-threshold mechanoreceptor.
Create a number variable called adaptation with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called clock with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called last_spike with initial value -1000.
Create a list variable called spike_times with initial value empty list.
For each frame in time_steps, perform the following operations.
    Create a number variable called drive with initial value 0.
    For each follicle in frame, perform the following operations.
        Create a number variable called gain with initial value 0.2.
        If the direction of follicle is equal to 1, then perform the following actions.
            Set gain to 1.0.
        End the conditional block.
        Set drive to the sum of drive and the product of the speed of follicle and gain.
    End the loop.
    Create a boolean variable called rested with initial value false.
    Set rested to the result of checking if the difference of clock and last_spike is greater than or equal to 2.
    If both the difference of drive and adaptation is greater than 1.0 and rested is true, then perform the following actions.
        Add clock to the end of spike_times.
        Set last_spike to clock.
        Set adaptation to the sum of adaptation and 0.6.
    End the conditional block.
    Set adaptation to the difference of adaptation and the product of adaptation and 0.067.
    Set clock to the sum of clock and 1.
End the loop.
Return spike_times to the calling function.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

# Python: plain arithmetic and lists let a non-programmer follow the logic.
def d_hair_spikes(frames, dt=1.0):
    """frames: one list per millisecond of (speed, direction) per follicle."""
    AGAINST, WITH_GRAIN = 1.0, 0.2   # against vs with the lie of the hair
    THRESH, TAU, REFRACT, STEP = 1.0, 15.0, 2.0, 0.6
    adapt, last, clock, out = 0.0, -1e9, 0.0, []
    for frame in frames:
        drive = 0.0
        for speed, direction in frame:          # pool the receptive field
            drive += speed * (AGAINST if direction == 1 else WITH_GRAIN)
        if drive - adapt > THRESH and clock - last >= REFRACT:
            out.append(clock)
            last = clock
            adapt += STEP                       # each spike deepens adaptation
        adapt -= adapt * dt / TAU               # adaptation leaks away
        clock += dt
    return out

if __name__ == "__main__":
    stroke = [[(2.0, 1), (1.5, 1), (0.5, 1)]] * 20
    still = [[(0.0, 1), (0.0, 1), (0.0, 1)]] * 20
    back = [[(2.0, -1), (1.5, -1), (0.5, -1)]] * 20
    print("against grain:", d_hair_spikes(stroke + still))
    print("with grain:   ", d_hair_spikes(back + still))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

A cell carrying TrkB and Cav3.2, wrapping a half-collar of lanceolate fingers around a fine hair, thinly myelinated yet more sensitive than any thick fibre, and firing only while that hair moves, is a D-hair cell and nothing else.

CHAPTER 245. C LOW-THRESHOLD MECHANORECEPTORS (C-LTMRS) (Caress-Speed Comfort Neuron) (NON-COG)

This is the neuron that makes a slow stroke down your arm feel good, and it is so slow that the pleasure arrives after the touch.

C-LTMR is an acronym built from fibre class and laboratory threshold, and it conceals one of the most human findings in sensory science.

Caress-Speed names the cell's tuning exactly, because it fires hardest to stroking at about one to ten centimetres per second delivered at skin temperature, and both faster and slower strokes reduce its firing, an inverted U-shaped curve that happens to match the speed of a human hand stroking another person.

Comfort names the faculty, since the firing rate of these unmyelinated afferents in hairy skin correlates directly with how pleasant people rate the touch.

Neuron marks it as a slow unmyelinated fibre, useless for discriminating shape, that exists chiefly to make being touched feel good.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

A C low-threshold mechanoreceptor, called a C-tactile afferent when recorded in awake people, is an unmyelinated cell tuned to gentle skin movement.

Single-fibre recordings show peak firing to stroking at 1 to 10 centimetres per second at a skin-warm 32 degrees Celsius, and at that temperature its firing rate tracks how pleasant the touch feels.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Like every ganglion cell it is pseudounipolar, a round soma on a forked stalk with no dendrites, ending in skin as a full collar of lanceolate fingers around a hair follicle.

Its bare axon lies grooved into a Schwann cell beside other bare axons, an arrangement called a Remak bundle, and ends centrally in a dense flame-shaped terminal at the core of a type II glomerulus.

SIZE:

The soma is small at 15 to 30 micrometres, the axon is 0.2 to 1.5 micrometres, thinner than a wavelength of red light, and the skin field is a spot of a few square millimetres.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

They are plentiful in hairy skin and sparse in hairless palm skin, found there at about one unit per eighty myelinated mechanoreceptors, roughly seven times lower density, and rodent work splits them further by TAFA4 and tyrosine hydroxylase content.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Somata occupy dorsal root ganglia from the second cervical to the sacral level, the interface where the peripheral nervous system meets the central nervous system.

Endings cover hairy skin of forearm, thigh, back and face, and central terminals sit in inner lamina II directly above the protein kinase C gamma interneurons, at the deep edge of the isolectin B4 band.

NUMBER:

A defensible range is 200,000 to 1,000,000 cells, mid-point near 500,000, about 5 per cent of the roughly 10 million human dorsal root ganglion neurons.

Confidence is low to moderate, since rodents give 10 to 15 per cent, human sequencing finds one small cluster, and no direct human count exists; that is some six neurons per million of the whole nervous system.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Resting voltage is near minus 55 millivolts and the spike is broad with a shoulder, the signature of C fibres, because its upstroke uses the slow sodium channel Nav1.8.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

It has no synapses on its soma; centrally it excites islet and protein kinase C gamma interneurons of inner lamina II inside glomeruli where several afferent profiles touch one another, and it receives axoaxonic contacts using gamma-aminobutyric acid and glycine.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Conduction is 0.5 to 1.5 metres per second, so a caress on the foot takes about a second to reach the cord, and repeated firing slows the axon further, a clinical signature called activity-dependent slowing.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

It uses glutamate packaged by the unusual vesicular glutamate transporter 3, adds tyrosine hydroxylase and the secreted protein TAFA4 in rodents, where TAFA4 damps inflammatory pain, and carries almost no calcitonin gene-related peptide.

MYELINATION:

Not Applicable, and the reason matters: this fibre is unmyelinated by definition, so it has no sheath, no nodes and no jumping conduction, although Schwann cells still cradle it inside Remak bundles.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Conducting without myelin costs more per millimetre because the whole membrane must be repolarised, but firing rates are low, and Remak Schwann cells supply lactate whose loss makes the longest foot fibres die back first.

PLASTICITY:

After injury spinal circuits let this gentle-touch input cross into pain pathways, TAFA4 shifts the state of spinal microglia, and stroke-to-stroke fatigue resets over tens of seconds.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Its distinctive partner is the Remak Schwann cell, which nurses many bare axons at once instead of insulating one.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Delete it and touch still works as information, since you would feel a hand, locate it and judge texture.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

This cell band-passes velocity, preferring middling stroke speeds and rejecting slower and faster ones, then multiplies that by thermal context so a skin-warm caress outscores a cold one.

It also releases the protein TAFA4 onto immune cells, sits in glomeruli where afferents contact each other, carries a memory of recent use as conduction slowing, and builds proteins locally in its far axon.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: stroke velocity in centimetres per second, useful range 0.1 to 100; contact force in millinewtons, threshold under 1; contact temperature 15 to 45 degrees Celsius; count of recent strokes.

• OUTPUTS: glutamate, via vesicular glutamate transporter 3, onto inner lamina II interneurons; TAFA4 protein onto local immune cells and microglia.

• STATE: membrane voltage; Piezo2 open fraction; Nav1.8 availability; fatigue accumulator; recent spike count driving conduction slowing.

• PARAMETERS: optimal velocity about 3 centimetres per second; optimal temperature 32 degrees Celsius; peak rate 30 to 60 impulses per second; conduction 0.5 to 1.5 metres per second.

• TIME CONSTANTS: transduction 1 to 5 milliseconds; spike 2 to 5 milliseconds; adaptation 0.5 to 2 seconds; fatigue recovery 10 to 60 seconds; foot to cord delay about 1 second.

• FAILURE MODE: in small fibre neuropathy these endings die back and pleasant touch dulls before pain does, and after nerve injury their input is rerouted into pain circuits, producing allodynia.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to compute the firing rate of a C low-threshold mechanoreceptor from stroke speed, contact temperature and recent use.
Create a number variable called rate with initial value 0.
Create a boolean variable called moving with initial value false.
Set moving to the result of checking if stroke_speed is greater than 0.
If moving is not true, then perform the following actions.
    Return 0 to the calling function.
End the conditional block.
Create a number variable called speed_score with initial value 0.
Set speed_score to the closeness of stroke_speed to 3 centimetres per second on a logarithmic scale, scaled from 0 to 1.
Create a number variable called warmth_score with initial value 0.
Set warmth_score to the closeness of contact_temperature to 32 degrees Celsius, scaled from 0 to 1.
Create a number variable called fatigue with initial value 1.
Repeat the following steps exactly recent_strokes times.
    Set fatigue to the product of fatigue and 0.85.
End the loop.
Set rate to the product of 50 and the product of speed_score and the product of warmth_score and fatigue.
Display rate to the console.
Return rate to the calling function.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:
