    odd = subunit(edge_pos, math.pi / 2)       # phases 90 degrees apart
    return math.sqrt(even * even + odd * odd)  # orientation energy

for pos in (-8, -4, 0, 4, 8):
    print("edge at", pos, "-> response", round(roaming_edge_response(pos), 2))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

It holds a steady tone for as long as the grating drifts.

Where its counterpart beats once per stripe, this cell fires at a constant rate no matter where the stripes happen to be, and that constancy is the whole point of it.

CHAPTER 298. COMMAND NEURONS (COG)

Startle a goldfish and one giant cell in its brainstem fires exactly once, throwing the whole animal sideways in five thousandths of a second.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

A command neuron is defined by an experiment, not by a shape.

Silence it and the behaviour disappears, fire it in its natural pattern and the whole behaviour appears unprompted, the two halves of the test Irving Kupfermann and Klaudiusz Weiss set in 1978.

Almost no single mammalian cell passes both, so real examples are small groups that pass together.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Broad dendrites, the branching input antennae of a neuron, gather many sensory streams, while one very thick axon splits into collaterals reaching dozens of downstream pools.

SIZE:

Human giant reticulospinal cell bodies measure 40 to 70 micrometres, a micrometre being one thousandth of a millimetre, and Betz cells, the cortical command elements for single muscles, reach 120.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

• Trigger commands fire once and release a fixed action, as in startle.

• Gating commands must keep firing throughout, as hypothalamic attack neurons do.

• Scaling commands grade the behaviour with their own rate, from approach to attack.

• Distributed command systems spread the job across a pool, the normal mammalian arrangement.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Human candidates sit in the gigantocellular reticular nucleus, the cuneiform and pedunculopontine nuclei of the midbrain locomotor region, the periaqueductal grey columns, the preBotzinger complex that triggers each breath, and the intermediate superior colliculus that triggers each flick of the eyes.

The decisive interface is the brainstem to spinal cord junction.

NUMBER:

Zero additional neurons.

Command neuron is a functional label on cells already counted as reticulospinal, periaqueductal, collicular and hypothalamic projection neurons.

Whether any human cell passes the test is unknown, since it needs single cell activation in a behaving person.

The best bound is the giant reticulospinal population, tens of thousands of cells, under one thousandth of one percent of the nervous system's roughly eighty six billion neurons.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Most sit below threshold with little spontaneous firing and then deliver one spike or a brief burst, while plateau potentials, depolarisations that outlast their input, let a gating command hold steady for seconds.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Input is a funnel and output a fan, and on the Mauthner cell of fish a feedforward inhibitory collar ensures that only one command wins and that it fires only once.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Human reticulospinal axons reach 10 to 15 micrometres across and conduct at 60 to 120 metres per second, because a command that arrives late is not a command.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Most use glutamate, the brain's main excitatory chemical messenger, though some are inhibitory, like the glycine using omnipause neurons that command the eyes to hold still.

MYELINATION:

Heavily myelinated, myelin being the fatty insulation made by oligodendrocytes that lets a signal leap between gaps instead of crawling.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

High peak demand and low average demand, because a large cell body with a very thick axon pumps many ions but fires rarely.

PLASTICITY:

The threshold is what learns, since repeated harmless stimuli strengthen the inhibitory collar, so a familiar bang stops making you flinch.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Glial processes form an axon cap around the Mauthner cell's output stalk, raising the resistance of the surrounding fluid so inhibitory cells can veto by field alone.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove the command elements and the region becomes a committee that deliberates forever, so breathing loses its trigger and the eyes drift instead of jumping.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

Ephaptic inhibition, a veto delivered by an extracellular electric field rather than a chemical, is the axon cap's trick, and gap junctions synchronise left and right partners so both cannot fire.

Corollary discharge, a copy of the command sent to sensory areas, marks the resulting sensations as self caused.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS.

◦ Sensory drive at 0 to 50 arbitrary units, minus a veto of 0 to 20.

• OUTPUTS.

◦ One all or none trigger to named follower pools, plus a corollary discharge copy.

• STATE.

◦ A drive accumulator and a lockout timer in milliseconds.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Threshold near 12 units, delay 3 to 15 milliseconds, fan out 20 to 200 pools.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Integration 10 milliseconds, lockout 200 to 400 milliseconds, gating seconds.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Freezing of gait in Parkinson's disease, where the legs work but the start signal never comes.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to decide whether a command neuron fires its single trigger.
Create a number variable called drive with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called lockout with initial value 0.
For each input in the sensory channels, perform the following operations.
    Calculate the sum of drive and the weight of this input.
    Set drive to the result.
End the loop.
Calculate the difference of drive and the veto.
Set drive to the result.
Check if lockout is greater than 0.
If that condition is true, then perform the following actions.
    Return no command to the calling function.
End the conditional block.
Check if drive is greater than or equal to 12.
If that condition is true, then perform the following actions.
    For each pool in the follower pools, perform the following operations.
        Display the trigger for this pool to the console.
    End the loop.
    Set lockout to 300.
End the conditional block.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

# Python is chosen because its plain syntax reads almost like the procedure above.
THRESH, LOCK_MS, TAU, DT = 12.0, 300.0, 0.010, 0.001
pools = ["flexor", "extensor", "postural"]
v, lock = 0.0, 0.0
for ms in range(600):
    # integrate, run the lockout down, then decide
    drive = 40.0 if 100 <= ms < 140 else 0.0  # a looming object arrives
    veto = 5.0 if ms > 300 else 0.0           # later cortical suppression
    v += (-v + drive - veto) * (DT / TAU)
    lock = max(0.0, lock - DT * 1000.0)
    if v >= THRESH and lock <= 0.0:
        v, lock = 0.0, LOCK_MS                # fire once, then refuse
        for p in pools:
            print("t=%d ms  COMMAND to %s" % (ms, p))
# One command reaches all three pools, milliseconds after the stimulus starts.

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

No other cell type is defined by an experiment rather than an appearance.

Switch one cell off and lose a behaviour, switch it on and conjure that behaviour from nowhere.

CHAPTER 299. SPARSE CODING NEURONS (Silent-Until-Recognized Neuron) (COG)

Somewhere in your memory system is a cell that has been silent all day and would fire hard at one particular face.

Sparse coding is a statistician's phrase about a population rather than a description a student can picture, so the new name describes what one such cell looks like on the electrode.

Silent names its resting condition, because in the human medial temporal lobe both lifetime and population sparseness are estimated at under two and a half percent, meaning any single cell says nothing at all to the overwhelming majority of pictures, names and sounds it is shown.

Until-Recognized names the moment it breaks that silence, which arrives only when one particular meaning is present.

Neuron marks it as a cell of recognition rather than raw sensation, because the same cell answers to a photograph of a person, to their written name, to their spoken name, and even to the person merely being recalled with nothing on the screen.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

Sparse coding neurons carry meaning by staying quiet, since at any instant only a tiny fraction of the population is active and each active cell stands for something specific.

Population sparseness means few cells answer one thing, lifetime sparseness means one cell answers few things, and the payoff is huge storage with little interference.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Dentate granule cells have a small round cell body and one narrow cone of dendrites facing the incoming fibres, so each admits only a thin slice of the input.

SIZE:

Dentate granule cell bodies are about 10 micrometres across, a micrometre being one thousandth of a millimetre, while nearby pyramidal cells run 15 to 25.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

• Pattern separating granule cells of the dentate gyrus.

• Concept cells of the human medial temporal lobe, answering one person across photographs and names.

• Silent majority cells, answering about one stimulus in a thousand.

• Sparse cortical pyramidal cells, most nearly mute and a few loud.

WHERE IT LIVES:

The dentate gyrus granule cell layer, hippocampal fields CA3 and CA1, the subiculum, entorhinal cortex, amygdala, perirhinal and parahippocampal cortices, and layer two of the piriform cortex.

The critical interface is the perforant path, where a dense cortical code is handed over and becomes a sparse hippocampal one.

NUMBER:

Zero additional neurons.

This is a functional label on cells already counted, chiefly the roughly fifteen million dentate granule cells and sixteen million CA1 pyramidal cells per human hippocampus.

Human recordings suggest one percent or fewer of medial temporal lobe neurons answer a given picture, and that groups for unrelated concepts share under one percent of their members.

Confidence is moderate, since those figures come from small samples biased toward active cells.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Dentate granule cells rest near minus 80 millivolts with low input resistance, so ordinary input does nothing, average rates stay far below one spike per second, and a cell that crosses threshold fires a vigorous burst.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Each granule cell needs many of its thousands of fibres to agree, basket cells then silence every neighbour, and the mossy fibre contact onto CA3 is a detonator that can drive its target with one spike.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Mossy fibres are bare and thin at about 0.2 micrometres, conducting at under one metre per second but compensating with giant swellings bearing dozens of release sites.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Glutamate is the transmitter, released alongside zinc, which modifies the receptors it lands on, and the opioid peptide dynorphin, which quietens the cell's own input.

MYELINATION:

Largely absent on mossy fibres, which is unusual for an output axon and reflects the very short distance travelled.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Low, and that is the design goal, because spiking dominates the brain's energy bill and two cells active in a hundred is far cheaper than a dense code.

PLASTICITY:

Mossy fibre strengthening happens in the sending terminal without the NMDA receptor, and human concept cells can take on a new concept within a day.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

The dentate gyrus holds a neurogenic niche, a pocket of stem like radial glia that can make new granule cells in adulthood at a rate still disputed in humans.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Delete the sparse coders and the hippocampus loses pattern separation, so today's parking space and yesterday's are written on the same cells and the second erases the first.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

Competition through inhibition turns a graded input into a discrete choice of winners, and dendrites act as subunits, so inputs must share a branch to count.

Homeostatic threshold adjustment regulates a population statistic, not a single cell, so sparseness is actively defended.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS.

◦ Thousands of weighted cortical afferents at 0 to 1, plus one global inhibition value.

• OUTPUTS.

◦ An active or silent flag, and when active a burst of two to seven spikes.

• STATE.

◦ A per cell adaptive threshold, a recent activity trace, and the weight vector.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Target active fraction one to three percent, homeostatic rate near 0.01.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Competition settles in 5 to 20 milliseconds, threshold drift takes hours to days.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Lose sparseness and the dentate gyrus overexcites toward temporal lobe epilepsy.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to choose the few cells that will represent the current input.
Create a list variable called drives with initial value empty list.
Create a number variable called target fraction with initial value 0.02.
Create a number variable called learning rate with initial value 0.01.
Create a number variable called input sum with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called best position with initial value 1.
Create a number variable called best drive with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called scan position with initial value 1.
Create a number variable called this drive with initial value 0.
For each cell in the population, perform the following operations.
    Set input sum to 0.
    For each input in the inputs of this cell, perform the following operations.
        Calculate the product of the weight of input and the value of input.
        Calculate the sum of input sum and the result.
        Set input sum to the result.
    End the loop.
    Calculate the difference of input sum and the threshold of this cell.
    Add the result to the end of drives.
End the loop.
Calculate the product of target fraction and the number of cells.
Set the quota to the result.
Repeat the following steps exactly quota times.
    Set best position to 1.
    Access the item at position 1 in drives.
    Set best drive to the result.
    Set scan position to 2.
    While scan position is less than or equal to the total number of items in drives, repeat the following steps.
        Access the item at position scan position in drives.
        Set this drive to the result.
        Check if this drive is greater than best drive.
        If that check is true, then perform the following actions.
            Set best drive to this drive.
            Set best position to scan position.
        End the conditional block.
        Calculate the sum of scan position and 1.
        Set scan position to the result.
    End the loop.
    Calculate the sum of the threshold of the cell at position best position and learning rate.
    Set the threshold of the cell at position best position to the result.
    Set the item at position best position in drives to the lowest possible value.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

# Python without libraries, because the winner takes all rule is plain arithmetic.
import random
random.seed(7)
N, M, FRAC, RATE = 500, 40, 0.02, 0.01     # cells, inputs, sparseness, homeostasis
W = [[random.gauss(0.0, 1.0) for _ in range(M)] for _ in range(N)]
th = [0.0] * N                             # per cell adaptive threshold
x = [random.random() for _ in range(M)]    # the input pattern
for trial in range(5):
    drive = [sum(w * xi for w, xi in zip(row, x)) - t for row, t in zip(W, th)]
    quota = max(1, round(FRAC * N))
    ranked = sorted(range(N), key=lambda i: drive[i], reverse=True)
    winners = {i for i in ranked[:quota] if drive[i] > 0.0}
    for i in range(N):                     # homeostasis defends the target
        th[i] += RATE if i in winners else -RATE * FRAC
    print("trial %d: %d of %d cells active" % (trial, len(winners), N))
# Ten cells in five hundred carry the pattern.

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

Near total silence punctuated by an emphatic and highly specific burst.

A cell that ignores a hundred pictures then fires to seven photographs of one person is a sparse coder.

CHAPTER 300. ERROR AND REWARD PREDICTION ERROR NEURONS (Prediction-Gap Reporting Neuron) (COG)

These cells do not report what happened, they report how wrong you were, and when the world matches your expectation exactly they say nothing at all.

The old label is a committee minute stacking two research literatures, error monitoring and reward prediction error, into one unspeakable string when both describe the same underlying job.

Prediction-Gap names the quantity the cell actually computes, the difference between what was forecast and what arrived, which in midbrain dopamine cells appears as a burst lasting roughly one hundred to two hundred milliseconds when the outcome is better than predicted, no change at all when it exactly matches, and a brief dip below baseline when it is worse.

Reporting names the purpose, since the signal is broadcast for others to learn from.

Neuron marks it as a cell whose human counterpart appears first in the pre-supplementary motor area and about fifty milliseconds later in dorsal anterior cingulate cortex, where the response predicts how much the person slows down on the next trial.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

A prediction error neuron computes the gap between what was expected and what arrived.

Midbrain dopamine cells burst when an outcome beats the prediction, sit at baseline when it matches, and pause when it falls short.

That is the learning signal of reinforcement learning, the branch of machine learning in which an agent improves by trial and error.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Midbrain dopamine neurons are medium sized and spindle shaped, with three to six sparse dendrites that can release transmitter themselves rather than via an axon.

SIZE:

Human dopaminergic cell bodies in the substantia nigra pars compacta run 20 to 35 micrometres and are stained dark by neuromelanin, visible as a black stripe in a fresh brain slice.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

• Reward prediction error dopamine neurons of the ventral tegmental area and substantia nigra.

• Negative signal neurons of the lateral habenula, excited by disappointment.

• Olivary teaching neurons, whose climbing fibres deliver a movement error to Purkinje cells.

• Performance monitoring error neurons of the pre supplementary motor area and cingulate cortex.

WHERE IT LIVES:

The substantia nigra pars compacta and ventral tegmental area, the lateral habenula, the rostromedial tegmental nucleus, the inferior olive, and the medial frontal cortex.

Two interfaces matter, the habenula to rostromedial tegmental junction that inverts a positive signal into a negative one, and the olive to cerebellum junction.

NUMBER:

Zero additional neurons.

These are labels on populations already counted, chiefly the roughly 400,000 to 500,000 dopaminergic neurons per side of the substantia nigra pars compacta.

The human olive count is not established, but with seven climbing fibres per olivary cell and fifteen million Purkinje cells, one to three million is a low confidence estimate.

Together they are under one hundredth of one percent of all human neurons, yet they gate learning brain wide.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

The dopamine cell paces at three to five spikes per second, then bursts to 20 or 30 for some 200 milliseconds on an unexpected reward and falls to zero when one is omitted.

The code is asymmetric, since a burst rises 25 spikes per second above baseline but a pause falls only five.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

Excitation from the pedunculopontine nucleus supplies the burst, inhibition from the rostromedial tegmental nucleus supplies the pause, and striatal neurons feed back the prediction.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

One human dopamine neuron carries an estimated four metres of axon and one to two million release sites, reaching much of a nucleus.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Dopamine, a monoamine acting through slow receptors rather than fast channels, with glutamate or gamma aminobutyric acid (GABA) released alongside it.

MYELINATION:

Dopamine axons are essentially bare, which is thought to be a major reason for their fragility.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Among the highest in the brain, because a bare four metre axon kept pacemaking by calcium entry carries a relentless oxidative load.

PLASTICITY:

These cells mostly cause plasticity elsewhere, the third factor telling a synapse whether a pairing was worth keeping.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

The substantia nigra carries one of the highest microglial densities in the human brain, and these immune cells react to neuromelanin spilled by dying neurons.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Remove them and the striatum still hears that something happened but never that it beat expectation, so habits freeze and the urge to move drains away.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

Dopamine is largely a volume transmitter, released into the extracellular space to reach cells it never touched, and it leaves dendrites as well as axons.

Measurements in awake human patients show striatal dopamine carrying at once what did happen and what would have happened otherwise.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS.

◦ Actual outcome value 0 to 1, minus the predicted value from striatal afferents.

• OUTPUTS.

◦ Firing rate with a floor of 0 and a ceiling near 30 spikes per second.

• STATE.

◦ A learned value estimate per cue and a running reward average.

• PARAMETERS.

◦ Baseline 3 to 5 spikes per second, learning rate 0.1 to 0.5.

• TIME CONSTANTS.

◦ Burst and pause 100 to 400 milliseconds, reuptake 100 to 500 milliseconds.

• FAILURE MODE.

◦ Parkinson's disease, where motor signs appear after roughly 30 percent of these cells are lost and 50 to 80 percent by diagnosis.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to turn a reward prediction error into a dopamine firing rate.
Create a number variable called predicted value with initial value 0.
Create a number variable called learning rate with initial value 0.3.
Create a number variable called positive gain with initial value 4.
Create a number variable called negative gain with initial value 1.5.
For each trial in the session, perform the following operations.
    Calculate the difference of the delivered reward and predicted value.
    Set the error to the result.
    Check if the error is less than 0.
    If that condition is true, then perform the following actions.
        Calculate the product of negative gain and the error.
    Otherwise, perform the following actions.
        Calculate the product of positive gain and the error.
    End the conditional block.
    Calculate the sum of 5 and that product.
    Display the result to the console.
    Calculate the product of learning rate and the error.
    Calculate the sum of predicted value and that product.
    Set predicted value to the result.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

# Python: the temporal difference rule is plain arithmetic.
BASELINE, CEILING = 5.0, 30.0   # measured tonic rate and phasic burst peak, in Hz
GAIN_POS, GAIN_NEG = 4.0, 1.5   # negative errors are compressed by the 0 Hz floor

def rate(rpe):
    """Turn a signed prediction error into a rate in spikes per second."""
    if rpe >= 0.0:
        return min(CEILING, BASELINE + GAIN_POS * rpe)
    return max(0.0, BASELINE + GAIN_NEG * rpe)

value, alpha = 0.0, 0.3         # learned prediction, then learning rate
for t in range(1, 9):
    reward = 0.0 if t == 6 else 1.0        # reward omitted on trial 6
    rpe = reward - value
    print("trial %d  error %+.3f  rate %5.2f Hz" % (t, rpe, rate(rpe)))
    value += alpha * rpe
# The burst fades as the cue is learned.

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

The silence on a correct prediction is the giveaway.

A cell that answers a reward at first, stops as it becomes predictable, then dips below baseline when it fails to arrive, is reporting an error.

CHAPTER 301. NOCICEPTIVE-SPECIFIC NEURONS (Injury-Only Warning Neuron) (COG)

This cell will ignore a stroking hand completely, and then erupt the moment the same skin is pinched hard enough to be damaged.

Nociceptive-specific is Latin-rooted jargon, built from a word meaning to injure, and it teaches nothing to a reader who does not already know the root.

Injury-Only names the threshold that defines the cell, since it stays quiet through brushing and warmth and speaks only when a stimulus reaches the range that threatens tissue.

Warning names the message, a report that damage is occurring and where, rather than a measurement of how much.

Neuron marks it as a dorsal horn cell, and the name is built to be read against the Touch-To-Damage Grading Neuron so the one dimension separating the pair, a threshold detector against a measuring instrument, is visible in both names.

WHAT IT IS AND WHAT IT DOES:

This is one of the two encoding classes of the spinal dorsal horn, the grey matter where body sensation first arrives.

It answers only stimuli strong enough to threaten tissue, and its job is to report danger and its location.

It is the alarm rather than the gauge, and its silence during ordinary contact is as important as its firing during injury.

STRUCTURE AND MORPHOLOGY:

Superficial cells of this class are spindle shaped, pyramidal or many sided, sitting in the topmost sheets of the dorsal horn.

Their dendritic trees stay largely within the superficial laminae where the thin damage-carrying fibres terminate.

SIZE:

Superficial projection neurons of this class are distinguished mainly by cell body size, above roughly 200 square micrometres in cross-section.

STRUCTURAL SUBTYPES:

• Projection neurons carrying the neurokinin 1 receptor, the docking site for substance P.

• Projection neurons lacking that receptor, which are larger and more densely excited.

• Marginal cells tuned narrowly to cooling, to warming or to itch.

• Silent neurons, recruited only after injury has already occurred.

WHERE IT LIVES:

Rexed lamina I, the marginal zone, and lamina II, the substantia gelatinosa, hold most cells of this class.

The same plan continues in the head as the spinal trigeminal nucleus caudalis, and the interface at which its inputs arrive is the dorsal root entry zone.

NUMBER:

Zero additional neurons, since this is a label on dorsal horn cells already counted among the anterolateral projection neurons and the far more numerous interneurons.

Human counts are not established, but in rat one mid-lumbar segment holds roughly 400 marginal zone projection neurons.

Scaling to a human is an order of magnitude guess, so the honest figure is tens of thousands of projection neurons carrying nearly all pain.

ELECTRICAL PROPERTIES:

Marginal zone cells fall into tidy firing classes, described as tonic, phasic, delayed onset and single spike.

Unlike its grading counterpart this cell does not show wind up, the progressive escalation of response to repeated identical stimuli.

SYNAPTIC CONNECTIONS:

It receives only the thin A delta and bare C fibres that carry damage signals.

It does not receive the thick A beta fibres of touch, and that exclusion is precisely what makes it specific.

AXON CHARACTERISTICS:

Projection axons cross the midline and climb the opposite anterolateral quadrant at 2 to 15 metres per second.

That range is why sharp and dull pain arrive at the brain separately rather than together.

NEUROTRANSMITTERS:

Glutamate is the output transmitter.

Receptor equipment rather than transmitter distinguishes this class, especially the neurokinin 1 receptor and the mu opioid receptor, the latter being the reason opioid drugs quieten this line so effectively.

MYELINATION:

Ascending axons carry a thin sheath at best, the price of packing many thin fibres into a narrow tract.

METABOLIC DEMANDS:

Modest at rest and steep under sustained noxious input, when these cells fire for minutes and switch on immediate early genes such as c-Fos.

PLASTICITY:

Part of the most plastic sensory relay in the body.

The gravest change is disinhibition, in which the chloride pump KCC2 is lost and gamma aminobutyric acid (GABA) begins to excite rather than inhibit, so that this alarm can be triggered by stimuli that should never reach it.

SUPPORT SYSTEM:

Dorsal horn microglia, the resident immune cells, drive nerve injury pain by releasing brain derived neurotrophic factor, the signal behind that loss of KCC2.

HOW IT COLOURS ITS REGION:

Delete this cell and the dorsal horn loses its alarm.

Injury would still register as intensity through the grading cells, but without urgency, so the body would know it was being damaged without being compelled to do anything about it.

BEYOND INTEGRATE AND FIRE:

Descending control from the brainstem lets an expectation formed in the frontal lobe silence this cell's response to an unchanged stimulus, which is the machinery of placebo analgesia.

Its silent subpopulation is recruited only by inflammation, so the cell class physically grows in size after injury.

SYSTEMS INTERFACE:

• INPUTS: A delta and C nociceptors at 0 to 30 spikes per second, plus descending modulation from the periaqueductal grey and rostral ventromedial medulla.

• OUTPUTS: spike count per stimulus from 0 to 60, to thalamus and parabrachial nucleus.

• STATE: membrane voltage, internal chloride concentration, descending gain factor.

• PARAMETERS: threshold set above the noxious range, no wind up accumulator, mu opioid receptor density high.

• TIME CONSTANTS: membrane 10 to 20 milliseconds, descending modulation seconds, chloride shift hours to days.

• FAILURE MODE: loss of KCC2 lowers the threshold so that touch fibres gain access to the alarm line, which is the cellular basis of allodynia, pain produced by an innocuous stimulus.

ENGLISH READABLE CODE:

The purpose of this procedure is to fire only when an incoming stimulus exceeds the threshold at which tissue is threatened, and to remain silent otherwise.
Create a number variable called stimulus intensity with initial value zero.
Create a number variable called noxious threshold with initial value seven.
Create a number variable called descending gain with initial value one.
Create a number variable called response with initial value zero.
For each stimulus in the sequence, perform the following operations.
    Set stimulus intensity to the measured intensity of that stimulus.
    If stimulus intensity is less than noxious threshold, then perform the following actions.
        Set response to zero.
    Otherwise, perform the following actions.
        Set raw response to the difference of stimulus intensity and noxious threshold.
        Set response to the product of raw response and descending gain.
    End the conditional block.
    Display response to the console.
End the loop.
The procedure is complete.

CODE SAMPLE:

# Python is chosen because a threshold detector is one comparison, and the contrast with grading is the point.
THRESHOLD = 7.0          # arbitrary intensity units: below this, tissue is not threatened
def injury_only(intensity, descending_gain=1.0):
    if intensity < THRESHOLD:
        return 0.0
    return (intensity - THRESHOLD) * descending_gain

for stim, label in [(1.0, "brush"), (3.0, "warm"), (6.0, "firm press"),
                    (8.0, "pinch"), (10.0, "noxious heat")]:
    print(f"{label:14s} intensity {stim:4.1f} -> response {injury_only(stim):5.2f}")

print("with descending inhibition at 0.3 gain:")
print("  pinch ->", round(injury_only(8.0, 0.3), 2))

WHAT MAKES IT UNMISTAKABLE:

Test it with a soft brush and it says nothing at all.

Then pinch the same patch of skin and it erupts, and that flat silence followed by sudden eruption is the signature no grading cell can imitate.
